<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> The separable quotient problem asks whether every infinite-dimensional Banach space has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient. We prove that this statement is independent of ZFC, relative to the consistency of ZFC with a measurable cardinal. This holds over both the real and complex fields. For spaces of norm density ℵ<sub>1</sub>, we obtain independence relative to the consistency of ZFC alone.

- **claim-main-independence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): For each $\mathbb F\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$, ZFC proves the following implications.* 1. *If $\mathfrak c$ is real-valued measurable, then $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ holds.* 2. *If the continuum hypothesis holds, then $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ fails.* *Consequently, if ZFC with a measurable cardinal is consistent, then $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ is independent of ZFC. Consistency of its failure requires only consistency of ZFC.
  - Explanation: In ZFC, a real-valued measurable continuum gives the separable quotient property, the continuum hypothesis refutes it, and the consistency strengths follow.
- **claim-small-density-independence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-intro-small-density)): If ZFC is consistent, then $\mathrm{SQ}^{\aleph_1}_{\mathbb F}$ is independent of ZFC for each $\mathbb F\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$.
  - Explanation: For spaces of norm density aleph one, independence from ZFC needs only the consistency of ZFC.
- **claim-prefix-normers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-prefix)): Let $(f_n)_{n\ge1}$ be a sequence in $X^*$ with $0<a\le\\|f_n\\|\le b<\infty$. Suppose that, for each $m\ge1$, a finite set $D_m\subset B_X$ satisfies $$\label{ev:normers} \max_{d\in D_m}\|v(d)\|\ge\tfrac12\\|v\\| \quad\bigl(v\in\operatorname{span}\{f_1,\ldots,f_m\}\bigr), \qquad f_n(D_m)=\{0\}\quad(n>m).$$ Then $(f_n)$ is a basic sequence whose prefix projections have norm at most $2$, and its coefficients satisfy $$\label{ev:coefficients} \|c_n\|\le\frac4a\left\\|\sum_i c_i f_i\right\\| \quad\text{for every finite sum.}$$ Put $G=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{f_n:n\ge1\}$, let $f_n^\#$ be its basis coefficient functionals, and put $Z=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{f_n^\#:n\ge1\}\subset G^*$. If $R_G(X)\subset Z$, then $R_G:X\to Z$ is onto. More precisely, its adjoint satisfies $$\\|R_G^*h\\|\ge\tfrac12\\|h\\|\qquad(h\in Z^*).$$
  - Explanation: Prefix norming sets with later vectors vanishing on them give a basic sequence with bounded prefix projections, bounded coefficients, and surjectivity of the evaluation map onto the coefficient-functional span.
- **claim-evaluation-criterion** (classification, [source](#stmt-ev-criterion)): For an infinite-dimensional real or complex Banach space $X$, the following are equivalent:* 1. *$X$ admits a bounded linear surjection onto a separable infinite-dimensional Banach space.* 2. *There is a closed infinite-dimensional subspace $F\subset X^*$ for which $R_F(X)$ is norm separable.* *In the second assertion, $F$ may be required to be separable.
  - Explanation: Having a separable infinite-dimensional quotient is equivalent to having a closed infinite-dimensional dual subspace with norm-separable evaluation image, which may be taken separable.
- **claim-small-restrictions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-small)): Let $T:Y\to Z$ be a strictly singular operator between real or complex Banach spaces. For every closed infinite-dimensional $M\subset Y$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a closed infinite-dimensional $N\subset M$ such that $\\|T\|_N\\|\le\varepsilon$. For a finite family of strictly singular operators on $Y$, the same $N$ can be chosen to satisfy this bound for every operator in the family.
  - Explanation: A strictly singular operator, or finitely many of them, has small norm on some closed infinite-dimensional subspace of any given closed infinite-dimensional subspace.
- **claim-measure-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-measure)): The cardinal $\kappa$ is regular. The measure $\mu$ is atomless, and for each positive integer $N$ the set $\kappa$ has a partition into $N$ sets of measure $1/N$.
  - Explanation: Under the measure hypothesis the continuum cardinal is regular, the measure has no atoms, and the space splits into equal-measure pieces.
- **claim-forced-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-density)): Let $X$ be a real Banach space with no separable infinite-dimensional quotient, and let $E\subset X^*$ be an infinite-dimensional separable closed subspace. Under the measure hypothesis above, the set $D=R_E(B_X)\subset B_{E^*}$ has norm density $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: If there is no separable infinite-dimensional quotient, the evaluation image of the unit ball on a separable dual subspace must have maximal density.
- **claim-null-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-family)): Let $X$ and $E$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#pos:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:density">7</a>. There are $\varepsilon>0$ and a family $(h_\alpha)_{\alpha<\kappa}$ in $S_{E^{**}}$ such that the functionals $f_\alpha=h_\alpha\circ R_E\in X^*$ satisfy $$\label{pos:seminormalized} \varepsilon<\\|f_\alpha\\|\le1\qquad(\alpha<\kappa),$$ and, for every $y\in R_E(B_X)$, $$\label{pos:pointwise-null} \mu\{\alpha:h_\alpha(y)\ne0\}=0.$$
  - Explanation: Maximal density yields a seminormalized dual family whose evaluation at each point of the image is nonzero only on a null set of indices.
- **claim-ordered-sampling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-sampling)): Let $X$ be a real Banach space, let $E\subset X^*$ be a separable closed subspace containing no isomorphic copy of $\ell_1$, and put $D=R_E(B_X)$. Let $I$ carry a countably additive probability $\mu$ on $\mathcal P(I)$ that vanishes on singletons. Suppose that $(h_\alpha)_{\alpha\in I}\subset E^{**}$ satisfies $\mu\{\alpha\in I:h_\alpha(y)\ne0\}=0$ for every $y\in D$, and that $f_\alpha=h_\alpha R_E$ satisfies $0<\varepsilon\le\\|f_\alpha\\|\le M<\infty$ for every $\alpha\in I$. Then $X^*$ contains an infinite unconditional basic sequence.
  - Explanation: Borel coding of bidual approximations and ordered iterated integration select an unconditional basic sequence in the dual from a pointwise-null seminormalized family.
- **claim-positive-theorem** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pos-theorem)): Suppose that $\mathfrak c$ carries a $\mathfrak c$-additive probability measure on its full power set that vanishes on singletons, with additivity understood for disjoint families of cardinality strictly less than $\mathfrak c$. Then every infinite-dimensional real Banach space has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient.
  - Explanation: A suitably additive measure on the continuum forces every infinite-dimensional real Banach space to have a separable infinite-dimensional quotient.
- **claim-positive-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-positive-transfer)): Let $X$ be an infinite-dimensional complex Banach space, and let $X_{\mathbb R}$ be its underlying real space. If $X_{\mathbb R}$ has a separable infinite-dimensional real quotient, then $X$ has a separable infinite-dimensional complex quotient.
  - Explanation: A separable quotient of the underlying real space of a complex space produces a complex separable quotient.
- **claim-finite-sum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-finite-sum)): If neither of two real Banach spaces $X_1,X_2$ has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient, then their direct sum has none.
  - Explanation: Absence of separable infinite-dimensional quotients is preserved by the direct sum of two real spaces.
- **claim-field-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ev-fields)): The universal separable quotient assertions over $\mathbb R$ and over $\mathbb C$ are equivalent. More specifically, if a real Banach space $X$ has no separable infinite-dimensional quotient, its complexification has no separable infinite-dimensional complex quotient.
  - Explanation: The universal assertions over the real and complex fields are equivalent, via the complexification of a real counterexample.
- **claim-parameter-choice** (construction, [source](#stmt-ch-parameters)): For each family there are strictly increasing sequences of positive integers $(L_j)$ and $(m_j)$, and positive integers $(d_j)$, such that, with $$\theta_j=\frac{L_j^{1/r}}{m_j},\qquad c_0=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\theta_j,\qquad B_j=2\sum_{h<j}L_h,$$ one has $$\begin{gathered} c_0<\frac18,\qquad \sum_{j\ge J}\theta_j\le4\theta_J,\qquad \sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac1{m_j}<\frac18, \label{ch:parameter-smallness}\\ \frac{\log(L_j/m_j)}{\log L_j}\longrightarrow\frac1s, \label{ch:parameter-growth}\\ (1/8)^{d_j}\le\theta_j,\qquad (1+B_j)^{d_j}\bigl(1+B_j+L_j^{1/q'}\bigr) \le\frac{L_j}{m_j}. \label{ch:parameter-depth} \end{gathered}$$ In particular, $L_j/m_j\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Growth, smallness and depth parameters for the norming operations can be chosen simultaneously for each family.
- **claim-coherent-closures** (construction, [source](#stmt-ch-closures)): For every $\beta<\omega_1$ there are nested finite sets $F_p(\beta)\subset[0,\beta]$, $p\ge1$, containing $\beta$, exhausting $[0,\beta]$, and satisfying $$\alpha\in F_p(\beta) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad F_p(\alpha)=F_p(\beta)\cap[0,\alpha]. \label{ch:coherence}$$ Consequently, two such closures with the same index $p$ that contain a common coordinate $\alpha$ have the same ordered initial segment through $\alpha$.
  - Explanation: Countable ordinals carry coherent finite closures, so supports can be recorded by order positions.
- **claim-norm-projections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-norm)): The formula $$\\|x\\|=\sup_{f\in K}\|f(x)\|\qquad(x\in c_{00}(\Gamma))$$ defines a norm. In its completion $E$, the coordinate vectors have norm one, and projections onto ordinal intervals and rectangles are contractive. Every vector has countable support and is a norm limit of finitely supported vectors. The corresponding pure norm on $C_k$ is given by $K_k$ and agrees with the full norm.
  - Explanation: The supremum over the norming set defines a norm whose completion has contractive interval and rectangle projections and a pure norm agreeing with the full norm on each color.
- **claim-coordinate-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-ch-coordinates)): For each $k$ and every finitely supported scalar family $(b_\alpha)_{\alpha\in C_k}$, $$\left\\|\sum_{\alpha\in C_k}b_\alpha e_\alpha\right\\| \le \\|(b_\alpha)\\|_{\ell_{s_k}}. \label{ch:ell-s}$$ Thus the same coordinate series converges in $E$ whenever $(b_\alpha)\in\ell_{s_k}(C_k)$.
  - Explanation: On each color the coordinate vectors satisfy an upper estimate by the corresponding sequence-space norm.
- **claim-coordinate-predual** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ch-predual)): Let $$X_0=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{e_\alpha^*:\alpha<\omega_1\} \subset E^*.$$ The canonical evaluation map $E\longrightarrow X_0^*$ is an isometric isomorphism.
  - Explanation: The space is the dual of the closed span of its coordinate functionals under the canonical evaluation map.
- **claim-path-bounded** (theorem, [source](#stmt-path-bounded)): For every actual infinite path $(\phi_l,j_l,p_l)_{l\ge1}$, the formula $$P(x)=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\phi_l(x)$$ defines a member of $B_{E^*}$. The sum converges for every $x\in E$. The same is true after an allowed crop of the path.
  - Explanation: An infinite path defines a functional of norm at most one, also after allowed crops.
- **claim-path-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-path-rigidity)): Distinct infinite paths in the same family have only finitely many weights in common. Consequently, for any finitely many distinct paths $P_1,\ldots,P_h$ in that family, one may delete finitely many initial pieces of each to obtain tails $\widetilde P_1,\ldots, \widetilde P_h$ satisfying $$\left\\|\sum_{b=1}^{h}a_b\widetilde P_b\right\\|_{E^*} \le\\|(a_b)_{b=1}^{h}\\|_{\ell_q} \qquad(a_b\in\mathbb R). \label{path:tail-estimate}$$ Each difference $P_b-\widetilde P_b$ belongs to $X_0$.
  - Explanation: Distinct paths share only finitely many weights, so after discarding finite prefixes finite combinations obey the Type II estimate.
- **claim-weak-cauchy-blocks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-path-cauchy)): Every bounded block sequence in a fixed family has a weak-Cauchy subsequence.
  - Explanation: A diagonal selection plus an oscillation contraction on the pointwise closure of the norming set yields weak-Cauchy subsequences.
- **claim-prescribed-weight-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pair-exact)): Let $(z_n)$ be an infinite admissible block sequence in $E$. Given a tail of this sequence and an integer $J\ge1$, there are a finite block $y$ in that tail and a Type I functional $\phi$ of weight $J$ in the same family such that $$\\|y\\|\le500,\qquad \phi(y)\ge\tfrac12.$$ The support of $\phi$ lies in the ordinal range of $y$, and also in its color range in the mixed case.
  - Explanation: In any tail of an admissible block sequence there is a bounded finite block detected by a Type I functional of a prescribed weight.
- **claim-block-partition** (bound, [source](#stmt-pair-partition)): For every integer $N\ge1$, every infinite admissible block sequence has a finite unit block $u$ such that, for all $1\le d\le N$ and all successive disjoint ordinal intervals $I_1,\ldots,I_d$, $$\sum_{b=1}^d\\|P_{I_b}u\\|\le8d^{1/r}.$$ The block can be chosen in any prescribed tail.
  - Explanation: There are unit blocks whose norm is spread over successive intervals with a controlled sum of projections.
- **claim-four-hits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pair-four-hits)): For every $\delta>0$ and every infinite set of indices there is an infinite subset on which no finite uncropped path has four hits at threshold $\delta$.
  - Explanation: An infinite Ramsey argument keeps uncropped paths from hitting four indices of a thinned set at a fixed threshold.
- **claim-subspace-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-branch-reduction)): Let $F$ be an infinite-dimensional closed subspace of $E$. There are a nonzero limit ordinal $\gamma\leq\omega_1$, an infinite-dimensional closed subspace $H\subset P_{<\gamma}E$, a bounded linear map $U:H\to F$, and one of the following two alternatives:* 1. *For some color $C_k$, we have $H\subset P_{C_k}E$ and $P_{C_k}P_{<\gamma}Uh=h$ for every $h\in H$.* 2. *We have $P_{<\gamma}Uh=h$ for every $h\in H$, and every restriction $P_{C_k}\|_H$ is strictly singular.* *In both alternatives, $P_{<\beta}\|_H$ is strictly singular for every $\beta<\gamma$. Moreover, if $P$ is an actual path functional supported below $\gamma$, and supported on $C_k$ in the first alternative, then $$\label{branch:lift-identity} P(Uh)=P(h)\qquad(h\in H).$$
  - Explanation: Any infinite-dimensional closed subspace is reduced to a subspace controlled by ordinal and color projections with a lifting map preserving path values.
- **claim-branch-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-branch-separation)): For every infinite-dimensional closed subspace $F\subset E$, there are a single operation family, a number $\delta_F>0$, and actual path functionals $P_b\in B_{E^*}$ of that family, indexed by $b\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb N}$, such that $$\\|P_b\|_F-P_c\|_F\\|\geq\delta_F \qquad(b\ne c).$$
  - Explanation: On every infinite-dimensional closed subspace, a binary tree of exact pairs yields continuum many path functionals separated by a fixed positive distance.
- **claim-twist-lemma** (theorem, [source](#stmt-twist-lemma)): Let $Y$ be a closed subspace of $E^*$ such that the evaluation map $E\to Y^*$ is an isometric isomorphism. Set $W=E^*/Y$ and let $Q:E^*\to W$ be the quotient map. Suppose that a bounded linear map $B:E^*\to W$ satisfies $$\\|B\\|\le\tfrac1{10},\qquad B^*(W^*)\subset J_E(E),\qquad B(E^*)\subset V:=\overline{B(Y)}.$$ Then $X=\ker(Q-B)$ is a Banach space, restriction induces an isomorphism $E\to X^*$, and $\overline{B(X)}=V$.
  - Explanation: A small perturbation of the quotient map with suitable adjoint range has a kernel that is a new predual with prescribed closure of the image.
- **claim-path-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-twist-path-map)): For each family $t$ there is a contraction $$S_t:\ell_{q_t}(\mathcal P_t)\longrightarrow W, \qquad S_te_P=QP.$$
  - Explanation: Path cosets define a contraction from a sequence space on the set of paths into the quotient.
- **claim-twist-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-twist-properties)): The operator $B$ satisfies $$\overline{B(X_0)}=\overline{\operatorname{span}} \{QP:P\in\mathcal P_t,\ t\text{ a family}\}=:V, \qquad B(E^*)\subset V, \qquad B^*(W^*)\subset J_E(E).$$
  - Explanation: The assembled operator has image closure equal to the span of path cosets and its adjoint lands in the canonical image of the space.
- **claim-ch-counterexample** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-twist-counterexample)): Under CH there is an infinite-dimensional real Banach space with no separable infinite-dimensional quotient.
  - Explanation: Under the continuum hypothesis the twisted predual has nonseparable evaluation images on every separable dual subspace, so no separable quotient exists.
- **claim-ch-density** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-twist-density)): Under CH, the real counterexample $X$ constructed above satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{dens}}X=\mathop{\mathrm{dens}}X^*=\aleph_1$. Its complexification is a counterexample over $\mathbb C$ of norm density $\aleph_1$.
  - Explanation: The counterexample and its dual have density aleph one, and its complexification is a complex counterexample of the same density.
- **claim-solovay-preservation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-model-solovay)): If $\kappa$ is real-valued measurable, then forcing with a measure algebra preserves its real-valued measurability. In particular, in the extension there is a $\kappa$-additive probability measure on the full power set of $\kappa$ in that extension, vanishing on singletons.
  - Explanation: Random forcing with a measure algebra preserves real-valued measurability of a cardinal.
- **claim-consistency-measurable-continuum** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-model-positive)): If ZFC with a measurable cardinal is consistent, then ZFC with a real-valued measurable continuum is consistent.
  - Explanation: From a measurable cardinal one obtains a forcing extension with a real-valued measurable continuum.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ch-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "For each family there are strictly increasing sequences of positive integers $(L_j)$ and $(m_j)$, and positive integers $(d_j)$, such that, with $$\\theta_j=\\frac{L_j^{1/r}}{m_j},\\qquad\n c_0=\\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}\\theta_j,\\qquad\n B_j=2\\sum_{h<j}L_h,$$ one has $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_0<\\frac18,\\qquad\n \\sum_{j\\ge J}\\theta_j\\le4\\theta_J,\\qquad\n \\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}\\frac1{m_j}<\\frac18,\n \\label{ch:parameter-smallness}\\\\\n \\frac{\\log(L_j/m_j)}{\\log L_j}\\longrightarrow\\frac1s,\n \\label{ch:parameter-growth}\\\\\n (1/8)^{d_j}\\le\\theta_j,\\qquad\n (1+B_j)^{d_j}\\bigl(1+B_j+L_j^{1/q'}\\bigr)\n \\le\\frac{L_j}{m_j}.\n \\label{ch:parameter-depth}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular, $L_j/m_j\\to\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-323.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ch-parameters",
          "exact_quote": "For each family there are strictly increasing sequences of positive integers $(L_j)$ and $(m_j)$, and positive integers $(d_j)$, such that, with $$\\theta_j=\\frac{L_j^{1/r}}{m_j},\\qquad\n c_0=\\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}\\theta_j,\\qquad\n B_j=2\\sum_{h<j}L_h,$$ one has $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_0<\\frac18,\\qquad\n \\sum_{j\\ge J}\\theta_j\\le4\\theta_J,\\qquad\n \\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}\\frac1{m_j}<\\frac18,\n \\label{ch:parameter-smallness}\\\\\n \\frac{\\log(L_j/m_j)}{\\log L_j}\\longrightarrow\\frac1s,\n \\label{ch:parameter-growth}\\\\\n (1/8)^{d_j}\\le\\theta_j,\\qquad\n (1+B_j)^{d_j}\\bigl(1+B_j+L_j^{1/q'}\\bigr)\n \\le\\frac{L_j}{m_j}.\n \\label{ch:parameter-depth}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular, $L_j/m_j\\to\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-323.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each family there are strictly increasing sequences of positive integers $(L_j)$ and $(m_j)$, and positive integers $(d_j)$, such that, with $$\\theta_j=\\frac{L_j^{1/r}}{m_j},\\qquad\n c_0=\\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}\\theta_j,\\qquad\n B_j=2\\sum_{h<j}L_h,$$ one has $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_0<\\frac18,\\qquad\n \\sum_{j\\ge J}\\theta_j\\le4\\theta_J,\\qquad\n \\sum_{j=1}^{\\infty}\\frac1{m_j}<\\frac18,\n \\label{ch:parameter-smallness}\\\\\n \\frac{\\log(L_j/m_j)}{\\log L_j}\\longrightarrow\\frac1s,\n \\label{ch:parameter-growth}\\\\\n (1/8)^{d_j}\\le\\theta_j,\\qquad\n (1+B_j)^{d_j}\\bigl(1+B_j+L_j^{1/q'}\\bigr)\n \\le\\frac{L_j}{m_j}.\n \\label{ch:parameter-depth}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular, $L_j/m_j\\to\\infty$.",
      "explanation": "Growth, smallness and depth parameters for the norming operations can be chosen simultaneously for each family.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ch-closures",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\beta<\\omega_1$ there are nested finite sets $F_p(\\beta)\\subset[0,\\beta]$, $p\\ge1$, containing $\\beta$, exhausting $[0,\\beta]$, and satisfying $$\\alpha\\in F_p(\\beta)\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n F_p(\\alpha)=F_p(\\beta)\\cap[0,\\alpha].\n \\label{ch:coherence}$$ Consequently, two such closures with the same index $p$ that contain a common coordinate $\\alpha$ have the same ordered initial segment through $\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-323.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-ch-closures",

          "source_id": "PM-323.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ch-norm",
        "exact_quote": "The formula $$\\|x\\|=\\sup_{f\\in K}|f(x)|\\qquad(x\\in c_{00}(\\Gamma))$$ defines a norm.       "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Distinct infinite paths in the same family have only finitely many weights in common.         "source_id": "PM-323.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-branch-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be an infinite-dimensional closed subspace of $E$. There are a nonzero limit ordinal $\\gamma\\leq\\omega_1$, an infinite-dimensional closed subspace $H\\subset P_{<\\gamma}E$, a bounded linear map $U:H\\to F$, and one of the following two alternatives:*\n\n1.  *For some color $C_k$, we have $H\\subset P_{C_k}E$ and $P_{C_k}P_{<\\gamma}Uh=h$ for every $h\\in H$.*\n\n2.  *We have $P_{<\\gamma}Uh=h$ for every $h\\in H$, and every restriction $P_{C_k}|_H$ is strictly singular.*\n\n\n*In both alternatives, $P_{<\\beta}|_H$ is strictly singular for every $\\beta<\\gamma$.         "source_id": "PM-323.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **kappa-additive measure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For an uncountable cardinal $\kappa$, a probability measure on $\mathcal P(\kappa)$ is *$\kappa$-additive* if it is additive on pairwise disjoint families of cardinality strictly less than $\kappa$; the sum over such a family means the supremum of its finite subsums.
- **real-valued measurable cardinal** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We say that $\kappa$ is *real-valued measurable* if it carries such a measure vanishing on singletons.
- **SQ at density aleph one** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $\mathrm{SQ}^{\aleph_1}_{\mathbb F}$ denote the assertion that every Banach space over $\mathbb F$ of norm density exactly $\aleph_1$ has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient.
- **evaluation map** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a closed subspace $F\subset X^*$, define the evaluation map $$R_F:X\longrightarrow F^*,\qquad (R_Fx)(f)=f(x).$$
- **strictly singular operator** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An operator is *strictly singular* if its restriction to no closed infinite-dimensional subspace is bounded below.
- **weak-Cauchy sequence** ([source](#sec:paths)): A sequence $(x_i)$ is weak-Cauchy if $(f(x_i))$ converges for every continuous linear functional $f$.
- **Martin's axiom at aleph one** ([source](#pm-h-37)): The axiom $\mathrm{MA}_{\aleph_1}$ states that, in any nonempty partial order with the countable chain condition, every collection of at most $\aleph_1$ dense sets is met by a filter.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-prefix** [claim](#stmt-ev-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-prefix): prefix norming sets, projection bounds, adjoint lower bound and series of corrections -> basic sequence and surjective evaluation
- **s-criterion** [claim](#stmt-ev-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-criterion): open mapping for one direction; inductive construction of annihilating unit vectors for the other -> evaluation criterion
- **s-small** [claim](#stmt-ev-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-small): inductive choice of vectors with tiny images and norming sets -> small restrictions
- **s-measure** [claim](#stmt-pos-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-measure): regularity from null small sets, atomlessness, divisibility by exhaustion -> regular atomless measure with equal partitions
- **s-density** [claim](#stmt-pos-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-density): Euclidean sections, random row selection and an expectation estimate against a small dense set -> maximal density of the evaluation image
- **s-family** [claim](#stmt-pos-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-family): separated set, transfinite enumeration and Hahn-Banach separation -> pointwise-null seminormalized family
- **s-sampling** [claim](#stmt-pos-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-sampling): Odell-Rosenthal coding, Borel Fubini, ordered measures and prefix-by-prefix selection -> unconditional basic sequence in the dual
- **s-pos-theorem** [claim](#stmt-pos-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-theorem): contradiction with the unconditional-sequence consequence of the Hagler-Johnson theorem -> positive implication over the reals
- **s-positive-transfer** [claim](#stmt-ev-positive-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-positive-transfer): real-linear isometry of duals and a case split on strict singularity -> complex separable quotient
- **s-finite-sum** [claim](#stmt-ev-finite-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-finite-sum): coordinate operators on a dual subspace, strictly singular or bounded below -> no separable quotient for the sum
- **s-fields** [claim](#stmt-ev-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ev-fields): complexification norm and identification with a direct sum -> equivalence over both fields
- **s-parameters** [claim](#stmt-ch-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ch-parameters): explicit choice of integer sequences with prescribed ratios -> parameter sequences
- **s-closures** [claim](#stmt-ch-closures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ch-closures): transfinite induction on countable ordinals -> coherent finite closures
- **s-norm** [claim](#stmt-ch-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ch-norm): crop stability by induction on construction stage and absolute convex hull comparison -> norm with contractive projections
- **s-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-ch-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ch-coordinates): Holder inequality on coefficient norms of norming functionals -> coordinate upper estimate
- **s-predual** [claim](#stmt-ch-predual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ch-predual): transfinite construction of a vector from a functional using Cauchy prefixes and averages -> predual identification
- **s-path-bounded** [claim](#stmt-path-bounded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-bounded): uniform boundedness of finite partial sums -> bounded path functionals
- **s-path-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-path-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-rigidity): injectivity of the coding map and position alignment -> almost disjoint weights and tail estimate
- **s-path-cauchy** [claim](#stmt-path-cauchy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-cauchy): diagonal selection, oscillation contraction on the compact closure and Riesz-Markov representation -> weak-Cauchy subsequence
- **s-pair-partition** [claim](#stmt-pair-partition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pair-partition): record index argument with Holder inequality -> spread unit blocks
- **s-pair-four-hits** [claim](#stmt-pair-four-hits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pair-four-hits): infinite Ramsey theorem and weak-star limit contradicting weak nullity -> thinned index set
- **s-pair-exact** [claim](#stmt-pair-exact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pair-four-hits-2): recursive estimate of norming expressions with depth cutoff and charge counting -> prescribed-weight pair
- **s-branch-reduction** [claim](#stmt-branch-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-branch-reduction): least ordinal where the initial projection is not strictly singular, then color projections -> reduced subspace with lift
- **s-branch-separation** [claim](#stmt-branch-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-branch-separation): block approximation, perturbation and a binary tree of exact pairs -> separated path functionals
- **s-twist-lemma** [claim](#stmt-twist-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-twist-lemma): iterated lifting, decomposition of the bidual and a norm-one quotient argument -> new predual
- **s-twist-path-map** [claim](#stmt-twist-path-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-twist-path-map): removal of finite prefixes, Type II rule and completion -> contraction on the path sequence space
- **s-twist-properties** [claim](#stmt-twist-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-twist-properties): finite approximation, label ranges and duality of sequence spaces -> properties of the perturbation
- **s-twist-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-twist-counterexample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-twist-counterexample): nonseparability of the image modulo a separable space using separated path restrictions -> counterexample under CH
- **s-twist-density** [claim](#stmt-twist-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-twist-density): counting dense sets and Hahn-Banach -> density and complex counterexample
- **s-model-solovay** [claim](#stmt-model-solovay) / [proof](#stmt-model-solovay): cited preservation theorem -> measurability in the random extension
- **s-model-positive** [claim](#stmt-model-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-positive): cardinal arithmetic of the measurable cardinal, chain condition counting and forcing -> model with measurable continuum
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#pm-h-36): combine both implications with the consistency of models -> relative independence
- **s-small-density** [claim](#stmt-intro-small-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-positive-2): Martin's axiom model giving the positive side and a CH model giving the counterexample -> independence at density aleph one

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-two-sided-zfc-implications**: For each scalar field, ZFC proves that a real-valued measurable continuum gives the separable quotient assertion, and that the continuum hypothesis refutes it. The assertion is therefore independent of ZFC relative to a measurable cardinal.
- **inv-evaluation-criterion**: An infinite-dimensional Banach space has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient exactly when some closed infinite-dimensional subspace of its dual has a norm-separable evaluation image.
- **inv-measure-gives-quotient**: A full power-set probability measure on the continuum that vanishes on singletons and has the stated additivity forces a separable infinite-dimensional quotient for every infinite-dimensional real Banach space.
- **inv-ch-counterexample**: Under the continuum hypothesis there is a real Banach space with no separable infinite-dimensional quotient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-pure-zfc-failure**: Assume in plain ZFC that some Banach space has no separable infinite-dimensional quotient. -> The counterexample is built only under the continuum hypothesis, and the opposite assertion holds in other models, so no ZFC-level conclusion is available.
- **anti-pure-zfc-positive**: Assume in plain ZFC that every infinite-dimensional Banach space has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient. -> The positive implication needs a measure on the continuum with the stated additivity, which is not provable in ZFC.
- **anti-strictly-singular-invertible**: Use strictly singular operators where a stable isomorphic embedding on subspaces is needed. -> Such an operator can be made small in norm on a suitable infinite-dimensional subspace, so it is never bounded below there.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are consistency and independence statements about all infinite-dimensional Banach spaces. They use the continuum hypothesis, a real-valued measurable continuum, transfinite constructions over the first uncountable ordinal, and forcing. These objects are infinite and nonconstructive, so no finite procedure computes them and no finite test or oracle can decide the statements.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves independence and existence results about Banach spaces. It gives no finite procedure that a program could run. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no dynamics, agents or physical model here. Only abstract spaces of nonseparable density are discussed, and these cannot be simulated. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The criterion for a separable quotient is a reformulation by evaluation images, not a checker that can be executed. A tool cannot test norm separability of an infinite family. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constructions use transfinite induction up to the first uncountable ordinal, uncountable path families and choice. They are not finite constructive procedures, so no pseudocode or complexity is meaningful. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coherent finite closures are indexed by countable ordinals and serve only as proof devices. They do not define an implementable structure with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the results concerns computation throughput or arithmetic circuits. The objects are infinite-dimensional and nonseparable. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no hardness assumption, primitive or security notion. Independence from ZFC is a statement about provability, not about computational hardness. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Learning settings use separable or finite-dimensional spaces, where a separable infinite-dimensional quotient always exists. The results concern only nonseparable spaces and offer no learning guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation program is formulated. The measure and density arguments are purely existential. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages or consensus notions here. The independence from ZFC is not a system property. |
| other | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The results are useful as a map of which separable quotient statements hold under which set-theoretic hypotheses. The theorem and corollary are indications for foundational work, not constructions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-ch-required** (hypothesis): The counterexample to the separable quotient assertion is constructed only under the continuum hypothesis. Its existence in plain ZFC is not claimed. Blocked use: ['Asserting in plain ZFC that some Banach space has no separable infinite-dimensional quotient', 'Using the counterexample in a model where the continuum hypothesis fails']
- **limit-measure-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The positive implication needs a full power-set probability measure on the continuum that vanishes on singletons. Additivity is only required for disjoint families of cardinality strictly below the continuum, and the theorem concerns real Banach spaces. Blocked use: ['Citing the positive theorem without its measure hypothesis', 'Reading the additivity as holding for families of size equal to the continuum']
- **limit-consistency-strength** (formal_scope): Independence is relative to the consistency of ZFC with a measurable cardinal. For spaces of norm density aleph one, only the consistency of ZFC is needed. Blocked use: ['Claiming independence from ZFC alone for the unrestricted assertion', 'Applying the density aleph one corollary to spaces of other density']
- **limit-evaluation-criterion-scope** (hypothesis): The evaluation criterion applies to infinite-dimensional real or complex Banach spaces. It reformulates the existence of a quotient and gives no procedure to decide it. Blocked use: ['Treating the criterion as an algorithm that checks separability of an infinite family', 'Applying it to finite-dimensional spaces']
- **limit-nonconstructive-selection** (nonconstructive): The positive argument proceeds by contradiction, using almost-everywhere selection with respect to a measure on a power set and the Odell-Rosenthal theorem. It produces no explicit sequence. Blocked use: ['Extracting an executable selection procedure from the sampling lemma', 'Assigning running times to the selection']
- **limit-transfinite-construction** (nonconstructive): The coordinate space is indexed by the first uncountable ordinal and uses coherent finite closures built by transfinite induction. Path families are uncountable, and their coding relies on the continuum hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Implementing the closures or path families as finite data structures', 'Using the construction without the continuum hypothesis for the coding of paths']
- **limit-scalar-field-transfer** (edge_case): The real positive theorem is transferred to complex spaces through the underlying real space. A real counterexample is transferred through its complexification, which relies on the finite direct sum lemma for real spaces. Blocked use: ['Transferring a counterexample by taking the underlying real space of a complex space', 'Skipping the complexification step']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 323 (`lean/docs/323.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Relative independence of the separable quotient problem](../../preprints/Relative-independence-of-the-separable-quotient-problem-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The separable quotient problem asks whether every infinite-dimensional Banach space has an infinite-dimensional separable quotient. The linked formalization proves the negative direction under the continuum hypothesis: over both the real and complex fields, there is an infinite-dimensional Banach space admitting no bounded linear surjection onto an infinite-dimensional separable Banach space.
>
> This is the conditional counterexample direction. The positive consistency direction and the paper's full relative-independence conclusions are outside the selected statement.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `CH` | def |
| `HasSeparableQuotient` | def |
| `SQ` | def |
| `negative_main` | theorem |

Comparator source `SeparableQuotientNegative.lean` (1001 bytes, sha256 `c79e5b43efcb76f5adc69e5ff6eb4b771db9f7cfe274c0e50bee65b4ff03d522`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace SeparableQuotient

open MeasureTheory Cardinal

universe u

/-- The continuum hypothesis. -/
def CH : Prop := Cardinal.mk ℝ = Cardinal.aleph 1

/-- A bounded linear surjection onto an infinite-dimensional separable Banach space. -/
def HasSeparableQuotient (𝕜 : Type) [RCLike 𝕜]
    (X : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup X] [NormedSpace 𝕜 X] : Prop :=
  ∃ (Y : Type u) (_ : NormedAddCommGroup Y) (_ : NormedSpace 𝕜 Y),
    CompleteSpace Y ∧ TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace Y ∧
      (¬ FiniteDimensional 𝕜 Y) ∧ ∃ T : X →L[𝕜] Y, Function.Surjective T

/-- The separable quotient assertion at an arbitrary ambient universe. -/
def SQ (𝕜 : Type) [RCLike 𝕜] : Prop :=
  ∀ (X : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup X] [NormedSpace 𝕜 X] [CompleteSpace X],
    (¬ FiniteDimensional 𝕜 X) → HasSeparableQuotient 𝕜 X

theorem negative_main (hCH : CH) : ¬ SQ.{u} ℝ ∧ ¬ SQ.{u} ℂ := by
  sorry

end SeparableQuotient

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


The separable quotient problem asks whether every infinite-dimensional Banach space has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient. We prove that this statement is independent of ZFC, relative to the consistency of ZFC with a measurable cardinal. This holds over both the real and complex fields. For spaces of norm density ℵ<sub>1</sub>, we obtain independence relative to the consistency of ZFC alone.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Every infinite-dimensional Banach space contains a separable infinite-dimensional closed subspace: take the closed span of a countable linearly independent set. The corresponding assertion for quotients is the *separable quotient problem*, traditionally attributed to Banach and Mazur. For a scalar field $\mathbb F\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$, let $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ denote the statement

> Every infinite-dimensional Banach space over $\mathbb F$ admits a bounded linear surjection onto a separable infinite-dimensional Banach space.

Equivalently, each such space $X$ has a closed subspace $M$ for which $X/M$ is separable and infinite dimensional. The equivalence follows by applying the open mapping theorem to the induced bijection from a space modulo the kernel of a surjection.

We work with the axiom of Choice throughout. Write $\mathfrak c=2^{\aleph_0}$ for the cardinality of the continuum. For an uncountable cardinal $\kappa$, a probability measure on $\mathcal P(\kappa)$ is *$\kappa$-additive* if it is additive on pairwise disjoint families of cardinality strictly less than $\kappa$; the sum over such a family means the supremum of its finite subsums. We say that $\kappa$ is *real-valued measurable* if it carries such a measure vanishing on singletons. In particular, the hypothesis below requires a measure on *every subset* of $\mathfrak c$, with this stronger additivity property.

The two implications below are proved in ZFC. Their hypotheses are then realized in models at the indicated consistency strengths; this gives the relative-independence conclusion.

<a id="stmt-intro-main"></a>
<div id="intro:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For each $\mathbb F\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$, ZFC proves the following implications.*

1.  *If $\mathfrak c$ is real-valued measurable, then $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ holds.*

2.  *If the continuum hypothesis holds, then $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ fails.*

*Consequently, if ZFC with a measurable cardinal is consistent, then $\mathrm{SQ}_{\mathbb F}$ is independent of ZFC. Consistency of its failure requires only consistency of ZFC.*

</div>

The CH counterexamples already occur at norm density $\aleph_1$, the smallest possible nonseparable density (Corollary <a href="#twist:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="twist:density">31</a>). At this density, an earlier positive theorem yields a sharper consistency conclusion. Let $\mathrm{SQ}^{\aleph_1}_{\mathbb F}$ denote the assertion that every Banach space over $\mathbb F$ of norm density exactly $\aleph_1$ has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient.

<a id="stmt-intro-small-density"></a>
<div id="intro:small-density" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *If ZFC is consistent, then $\mathrm{SQ}^{\aleph_1}_{\mathbb F}$ is independent of ZFC for each $\mathbb F\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$.*

</div>

The positive side of this corollary uses the density criterion of Saxon and Sánchez Ruiz described below, together with Martin’s axiom. The negative side uses the counterexample constructed here. The measurable cardinal hypothesis in Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a> concerns the unrestricted quotient assertion.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### History and methods.

The history and many positive cases of the problem are surveyed in (Brech 2025; Ferrando et al. 2018) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Brech2025](#ref-Brech2025), [FerrandoKakolLopezPellicerSliwa2018](#ref-FerrandoKakolLopezPellicerSliwa2018)]. Brech notes that the problem is not stated explicitly in Banach’s 1932 book and identifies Rosenthal’s 1969 paper as an early written formulation (Brech 2025, 74) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Brech2025](#ref-Brech2025)]. Johnson and Rosenthal developed the weak-star basic-sequence method and proved that every separable infinite-dimensional Banach space has an infinite-dimensional quotient with a Schauder basis (Johnson and Rosenthal 1972) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[JohnsonRosenthal1972](#ref-JohnsonRosenthal1972)]. Argyros, Dodos and Kanellopoulos proved the positive answer for every dual Banach space (Argyros, Dodos, and Kanellopoulos 2008, Theorem 15) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ArgyrosDodosKanellopoulos2008](#ref-ArgyrosDodosKanellopoulos2008)]. Their Proposition 16, based on work of Hagler and Johnson (Hagler and Johnson 1977) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[HaglerJohnson1977](#ref-HaglerJohnson1977)], also shows that an unconditional basic sequence in $X^*$ gives a separable infinite-dimensional quotient of $X$ with an unconditional basis. This implication is central to our positive argument. Morris and Yost give a common sufficient condition covering both dual spaces and spaces that are the closed linear span of a weakly compact subset (Morris and Yost 2020) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MorrisYost2020](#ref-MorrisYost2020)]. More recently, Patri states a $c_0$-quotient criterion for $\mathcal L_\infty$ spaces admitting an infinite-dimensional Schur quotient, in which weakly convergent sequences converge in norm. He applies the criterion to a class of nonseparable Bourgain–Pisier spaces (Patri 2026) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Patri2026](#ref-Patri2026)].

Earlier set-theoretic positive results depend on the density of the space. Write $\mathfrak b$ for the least cardinality of a family in $\mathbb N^\mathbb N$ unbounded under eventual domination, where $f\leq^*g$ means $f(n)\leq g(n)$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Saxon and Sánchez Ruiz proved that every infinite-dimensional Banach space of density less than $\mathfrak b$ has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient (Saxon and Sánchez Ruiz 1996) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[SaxonSanchezRuiz1996](#ref-SaxonSanchezRuiz1996)]; see also (Brech 2025, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Brech2025](#ref-Brech2025)]. In particular, the positive assertion at density $\aleph_1$ already follows under $\mathfrak b>\aleph_1$. For large densities, Dodos, Lopez-Abad and Todorčević proved, relative to infinitely many strongly compact cardinals, consistency with GCH of a positive answer for every Banach space of density at least $\aleph_\omega$ (Dodos, Lopez-Abad, and Todorčević 2011) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DodosLopezAbadTodorcevic2011](#ref-DodosLopezAbadTodorcevic2011)]. The positive implication here applies to every infinite-dimensional Banach space. The negative construction uses conditional norming operations in the tradition of Gowers–Maurey (Gowers and Maurey 1993) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GowersMaurey1993](#ref-GowersMaurey1993)], branch functionals as in James-tree constructions (James 1974; Argyros, Arvanitakis, and Tolias 2006) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[James1974](#ref-James1974), [ArgyrosArvanitakisTolias2006](#ref-ArgyrosArvanitakisTolias2006)], and coding by order positions in coherent finite ordinal closures (Argyros, Lopez-Abad, and Todorčević 2005, sec. 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ArgyrosLopezAbadTodorcevic2005](#ref-ArgyrosLopezAbadTodorcevic2005)]. We give the particular construction and all its estimates in full.

We write $B_Z$ and $S_Z$ for the closed unit ball and unit sphere of a Banach space $Z$, and $[z_i:i\in I]$ for a norm-closed linear span. All separability and density assertions about subsets of dual spaces refer to the norm topology unless another topology is specified.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The common criterion.

For a closed subspace $F\subset X^*$, define the evaluation map $$R_F:X\longrightarrow F^*,\qquad (R_Fx)(f)=f(x).$$ A separable infinite-dimensional quotient exists precisely when some infinite-dimensional closed $F$ has norm-separable evaluation image. In the necessary direction $F$ may itself be chosen separable. Section <a href="#sec:evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:evaluation">2</a> proves this criterion, including the surjectivity step. The two constructions in the paper use this same criterion in opposite ways.

For the positive implication, start with a putative counterexample $X$ and a separable infinite-dimensional subspace $E\subset X^*$. The measure first forces $R_E(B_X)$ to have density $\mathfrak c$: a smaller dense set would allow simultaneous summable evaluations on a suitable basic sequence, and hence a separable quotient. From the large evaluation image we construct a seminormalized family in $X^*$ which vanishes almost everywhere at each point of $X$. Odell–Rosenthal supplies sequential bidual approximations. Coding these approximations lets us use ordinary Borel Fubini to select an unconditional basic sequence from that family. This contradicts the Hagler–Johnson consequence above. The Borel coding is what permits the integration argument; no interchange theorem for arbitrary power-set measures is needed.

For the negative implication, we build a dual space $E=X_0^*$ with coordinate vectors indexed by $\omega_1$. We construct paths from successive finitely supported functionals with bounded partial sums; each path therefore defines a functional on $E$. Each piece carries an integer weight. The coding makes distinct paths share only finitely many weights, yielding uniform estimates for combinations of their tails. A block argument then gives, on every infinite-dimensional subspace of $E$, uncountably many norm-separated restrictions of path functionals. CH lets us assign all these paths to coordinates of $E$. A small perturbation of the quotient map $E^*\to E^*/X_0$ produces a new predual $X$ whose evaluations approximate all path cosets. Its evaluation image on every separable infinite-dimensional subspace of $X^*\simeq E$ is nonseparable, excluding all separable infinite-dimensional quotients. The perturbation lemma in Section <a href="#sec:predual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:predual">9</a> isolates this change of predual from the combinatorial construction.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Organization.

Section <a href="#sec:evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:evaluation">2</a> develops the evaluation criterion and a selection lemma for strictly singular operators. Section <a href="#sec:positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positive">[sec:positive]</a> proves the positive implication over $\mathbb R$, and Section <a href="#sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fields">4</a> transfers it to $\mathbb C$ while preparing the corresponding transfer for counterexamples. Sections <a href="#sec:norming" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norming">5</a>–<a href="#sec:branches" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:branches">[sec:branches]</a> construct the coordinate dual, prove convergence for its block sequences, establish the required block estimates, and separate functionals on arbitrary subspaces. Section <a href="#sec:predual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:predual">9</a> changes its predual and proves the CH counterexample. Section <a href="#model:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:section">10</a> supplies the set-theoretic models and proves both Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a> and the sharper restricted-density Corollary <a href="#intro:small-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:small-density">2</a>.

<a id="sec:evaluation"></a>
### Separable evaluation images

The positive and negative arguments will use the same test for the existence of a separable quotient. Let $X$ be a Banach space over $\mathbb K\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$, and write $B_X$ for its closed unit ball. For a closed subspace $F\subset X^*$, define the evaluation operator $$R_F:X\longrightarrow F^*,\qquad (R_Fx)(f)=f(x).$$ The relevant condition is norm separability of $R_F(X)$, rather than separability of $F$ itself. We first explain why this condition yields an actual quotient.

<a id="stmt-ev-prefix"></a>
<div id="ev:prefix" class="lemma">

<a id="ev:coefficients"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Prefix normers and surjectivity). *Let $(f_n)_{n\ge1}$ be a sequence in $X^*$ with $0<a\le\|f_n\|\le b<\infty$. Suppose that, for each $m\ge1$, a finite set $D_m\subset B_X$ satisfies <a id="ev:normers"></a>$$\label{ev:normers}
 \max_{d\in D_m}|v(d)|\ge\tfrac12\|v\|
 \quad\bigl(v\in\operatorname{span}\{f_1,\ldots,f_m\}\bigr),
 \qquad f_n(D_m)=\{0\}\quad(n>m).$$ Then $(f_n)$ is a basic sequence whose prefix projections have norm at most $2$, and its coefficients satisfy $$\label{ev:coefficients}
 |c_n|\le\frac4a\left\|\sum_i c_i f_i\right\|
 \quad\text{for every finite sum.}$$ Put $G=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{f_n:n\ge1\}$, let $f_n^\#$ be its basis coefficient functionals, and put $Z=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{f_n^\#:n\ge1\}\subset G^*$. If $R_G(X)\subset Z$, then $R_G:X\to Z$ is onto. More precisely, its adjoint satisfies $$\|R_G^*h\|\ge\tfrac12\|h\|\qquad(h\in Z^*).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ev-prefix"><summary>Proof of stmt-ev-prefix</summary>

*Proof.* The vectors are linearly independent: a vector $f_n$ in the preceding span would vanish on its norming set $D_{n-1}$ and hence be zero. For $v=\sum_{i=1}^N c_i f_i$ and $m\le N$, the values of $v$ on $D_m$ equal those of its prefix. Thus $$\left\|\sum_{i=1}^m c_i f_i\right\|
 \le2\max_{d\in D_m}|v(d)|\le2\|v\|.$$ The prefix projections extend to $G$ and converge to the identity there, because they do so on the dense subspace of finite sums. Taking differences of two consecutive prefixes gives <a href="#ev:coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ev:coefficients">[ev:coefficients]</a>.

Write $T=R_G:X\to Z$. For $h\in Z^*$, set $$v_m=\sum_{i=1}^m h(f_i^\#)f_i\in G.$$ If $d\in D_m$, then $Td=\sum_{i=1}^m f_i(d)f_i^\#$, so $(T^*h)(d)=v_m(d)$. Consequently $\|T^*h\|\ge\|v_m\|/2$. For every finite linear combination $u$ of the coefficient functionals, we have $h(u)=u(v_m)$ for all sufficiently large $m$. Since these combinations are dense in $Z$, $$\|h\|\le\sup_m\|v_m\|\le2\|T^*h\|.$$ This argument does not require the vectors $v_m$ to converge.

We recall why an adjoint lower bound gives surjectivity. If $\|T^*z^*\|\ge c\|z^*\|$ for every $z^*\in Z^*$, Hahn–Banach separation of the balanced convex set $\overline{T(B_X)}$ gives $cB_Z\subset\overline{T(B_X)}$. For a residual $z\ne0$, choose $x\in X$ with $\|x\|\le\|z\|/c$ and $\|z-Tx\|\le\|z\|/2$. Repeating this step makes the residuals decrease geometrically and the chosen vectors form an absolutely convergent series in $X$. Its sum maps to the original $z$ and has norm at most $2\|z\|/c$. Apply this with $c=1/2$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-ev-criterion"></a>
<div id="ev:criterion" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Evaluation criterion). *For an infinite-dimensional real or complex Banach space $X$, the following are equivalent:*

1.  *$X$ admits a bounded linear surjection onto a separable infinite-dimensional Banach space.*

2.  *There is a closed infinite-dimensional subspace $F\subset X^*$ for which $R_F(X)$ is norm separable.*

*In the second assertion, $F$ may be required to be separable.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ev-criterion"><summary>Proof of stmt-ev-criterion</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $T:X\to Y$ is such a surjection. Choose a closed separable infinite-dimensional subspace $U\subset Y^*$. The open mapping theorem makes $T^*$ bounded below, so $F=T^*U$ is a closed separable infinite-dimensional subspace of $X^*$. Under the isomorphism $T^*|_U:U\to F$, evaluation at $x$ becomes evaluation at $Tx$. The evaluation map $Y\to U^*$ is bounded and $Y$ is separable; hence $R_F(X)$ is norm separable.

Conversely, assume the second assertion and choose $x_j\in X$ so that $\{R_Fx_j:j\ge1\}$ is norm dense in $R_F(X)$. We construct $f_n\in F$ of norm one. At stage $n$, require $f_n$ to annihilate $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ and all finite sets $D_m$ already chosen, $m<n$. These conditions define a finite-codimensional subspace of $F$, so a unit vector satisfying them exists. After choosing $f_n$, choose $D_n\subset B_X$ that is $1/2$-norming for $\operatorname{span}\{f_1,\ldots,f_n\}$. Such a finite set exists by compactness of the finite-dimensional unit sphere and the definition of the dual norm.

Use the spaces $G$ and $Z$ from Lemma <a href="#ev:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:prefix">3</a>. For each $j$, $$R_Gx_j=\sum_{i<j}f_i(x_j)f_i^\#\in Z.$$ Restriction from $F^*$ to $G^*$ is contractive. Density of the evaluations at the $x_j$, and closedness of $Z$, therefore give $R_G(X)\subset Z$. Lemma <a href="#ev:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:prefix">3</a> makes $R_G:X\to Z$ onto. The coefficient functionals are linearly independent, so $Z$ is separable and infinite-dimensional. The space $G$ also supplies the separable witness in the second assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Small restrictions of strictly singular operators

We will use one elementary selection principle both in changing scalars and in the later construction on arbitrary subspaces. An operator is *strictly singular* if its restriction to no closed infinite-dimensional subspace is bounded below.

<a id="stmt-ev-small"></a>
<div id="ev:small" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *Let $T:Y\to Z$ be a strictly singular operator between real or complex Banach spaces. For every closed infinite-dimensional $M\subset Y$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a closed infinite-dimensional $N\subset M$ such that $\|T|_N\|\le\varepsilon$. For a finite family of strictly singular operators on $Y$, the same $N$ can be chosen to satisfy this bound for every operator in the family.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ev-small"><summary>Proof of stmt-ev-small</summary>

*Proof.* Inductively choose unit vectors $y_n\in M$ and finite $1/2$-norming sets in $B_{Y^*}$ for their prefix spans. Require each new vector to annihilate all earlier norming sets and to satisfy $\|Ty_n\|<\varepsilon 2^{-n}/4$. The annihilation conditions leave an infinite-dimensional closed subspace; strict singularity says that $T$ is not bounded below there, so the choice is possible. The prefix calculation in Lemma <a href="#ev:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:prefix">3</a>, with vectors and norming functionals interchanged, shows that $(y_n)$ is basic and $|a_n|\le4\|\sum_i a_i y_i\|$. Therefore $$\left\|T\sum_n a_n y_n\right\|
 \le4\sum_n\|Ty_n\|\left\|\sum_n a_n y_n\right\|
 \le\varepsilon\left\|\sum_n a_n y_n\right\|.$$ Continuity gives the bound on $N=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{y_n:n\ge1\}$. For finitely many operators, apply the result successively inside the subspace already obtained. Restrictions of strictly singular operators remain strictly singular, and previous bounds persist on smaller subspaces. ◻

</details>

<a id="pos:section"></a>
### A measure on the continuum gives separable quotients

<span id="sec:positive" label="sec:positive"></span>

We prove the positive implication over the real field. The argument has two steps. First we show that failure of a separable infinite-dimensional quotient forces an evaluation image of maximal density, and use that image to construct a dual family whose evaluation at each fixed vector is almost surely zero. We then code the family by sequences in a separable space and select an infinite sequence satisfying all finite suppression estimates.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The measure hypothesis

Throughout this section let $\kappa=\mathfrak c=2^{\aleph_0}$, regarded as its initial ordinal. Assume that $\mu$ is a probability measure on $\mathcal P(\kappa)$, vanishes on singletons, and is *$\kappa$-additive* in the following convention: for every disjoint family $(A_\xi)_{\xi<\lambda}$ with $\lambda<\kappa$, $$\mu\Bigl(\bigcup_{\xi<\lambda}A_\xi\Bigr)
   =\sum_{\xi<\lambda}\mu(A_\xi),$$ where a sum of nonnegative terms is the supremum of its finite subsums. In particular, $\mu$ is countably additive, and a union of fewer than $\kappa$ null sets is null: disjointify such a family before applying the displayed identity. No additivity assertion for families of size $\kappa$ is intended.

<a id="stmt-pos-measure"></a>
<div id="pos:measure" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *The cardinal $\kappa$ is regular. The measure $\mu$ is atomless, and for each positive integer $N$ the set $\kappa$ has a partition into $N$ sets of measure $1/N$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pos-measure"><summary>Proof of stmt-pos-measure</summary>

*Proof.* Every set of cardinality less than $\kappa$ is null. If $\operatorname{cf}(\kappa)<\kappa$, a cofinal family of fewer than $\kappa$ initial segments would therefore express $\kappa$ as a null set.

Fix an injection $b:\kappa\to\{0,1\}^{\mathbb N}$. If $A$ were a positive atom, then for each successive bit one of the two cells would have full measure in the preceding cell of $A$. This gives decreasing subsets $A_n\subset A$ of measure $\mu(A)$ on which the first $n$ bits are fixed. Their intersection has at most one point, whereas countable additivity gives it measure $\mu(A)>0$. This contradiction proves atomlessness.

We include the elementary divisibility argument. Every positive set has a positive subset of measure at most any given $\varepsilon>0$: repeatedly split a positive set into two positive sets and retain the smaller part. A maximal disjoint family of such small positive subsets of a measurable set $A$ is countable, since the measure is finite, and covers $A$ modulo a null set. For $0\le t\le\mu(A)$, successive unions of these pieces either attain measure $t$, converge upwards to $t$, or first cross $t$. In the last case the union before that crossing has measure in $(t-\varepsilon,t)$. Thus one can approximate $t$ from below with error at most $\varepsilon$. Apply this construction repeatedly in the remainder of $A$, with errors tending to zero, and take the union. It has measure exactly $t$. Removing sets of measure $1/N$ one at a time gives the required partition. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The density forced by failure of a quotient

The evaluation operator $R_E:X\to E^*$ for $E\subset X^*$ is as in Proposition <a href="#ev:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:criterion">4</a>. The following argument uses the finite-dimensional Euclidean sections supplied by Dvoretzky’s Theorem (Dvoretzky 1961; Figiel 1976) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Dvoretzky1961](#ref-Dvoretzky1961), [Figiel1976](#ref-Figiel1976)].

<a id="stmt-pos-density"></a>
<div id="pos:density" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7**. *Let $X$ be a real Banach space with no separable infinite-dimensional quotient, and let $E\subset X^*$ be an infinite-dimensional separable closed subspace. Under the measure hypothesis above, the set $D=R_E(B_X)\subset B_{E^*}$ has norm density $\kappa$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pos-density"><summary>Proof of stmt-pos-density</summary>

*Proof.* Every member of $E^*$ is determined by its values on a fixed countable dense subset of $E$, so $|D|\le\mathfrak c=\kappa$. Suppose that $A\subset D$ is norm dense and $|A|<\kappa$.

<a id="pos:rows"></a>
For $n\ge1$ put $N_n=16^n$. Inductively choose vectors $(u_{n,j})_{j=1}^{N_n}\subset E$ such that $$\label{pos:rows}
 \|a\|_2\le\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{N_n}a_j u_{n,j}\Bigr\|
       \le2\|a\|_2\qquad(a\in\mathbb R^{N_n}).$$ After choosing row $n$, choose a finite set $D_n\subset B_X$ that $1/2$-norms the span of all rows up to $n$. Require every subsequent row to annihilate $D_n$. At each step the finitely many annihilation conditions leave an infinite-dimensional closed subspace of $E$, so Dvoretzky’s Theorem gives <a href="#pos:rows" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:rows">[pos:rows]</a> there.

For each $n$, Lemma <a href="#pos:measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:measure">6</a> gives a map $j_n:\kappa\to\{1,\ldots,N_n\}$ whose fibers have measure $1/N_n$. For $y\in A$, <a href="#pos:rows" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:rows">[pos:rows]</a> implies $\|(y(u_{n,j}))_{j=1}^{N_n}\|_2\le2$, and hence $$\int_\kappa |y(u_{n,j_n(\alpha)})|\,d\mu(\alpha)
 \le \frac2{\sqrt{N_n}}.$$ The sum of these expectations is finite. Monotone convergence shows that $\sum_n|y(u_{n,j_n(\alpha)})|<\infty$ for almost every $\alpha$. Intersecting these conull sets over $y\in A$ is valid because $|A|<\kappa$. Choose one $\alpha$ in their intersection and set $g_n=u_{n,j_n(\alpha)}$.

The vectors $g_n$ have norms between $1$ and $2$, and the sets $D_n$ are actual half-normers of their prefix spans, annihilated by all later vectors. Lemma <a href="#ev:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:prefix">3</a> therefore makes $(g_n)$ basic, with coefficient functionals $g_n^\#\in G^*$ of norm at most $4$, where $G=[g_n:n\ge1]$. Set $Z=[g_n^\#:n\ge1]\subset G^*$. For $y\in A$, the series $\sum_n y(g_n)g_n^\#$ converges in $G^*$, since its tail has norm at most $4\sum_{n>m}|y(g_n)|$. On the dense span of the $g_n$ its sum agrees with $y|_G$, so $y|_G\in Z$. Restriction is contractive and $A$ is dense in $D$, whence $R_G(B_X)\subset Z$. Scaling gives $R_G(X)\subset Z$. Lemma <a href="#ev:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ev:prefix">3</a> now says that $R_G:X\to Z$ is onto, contradicting the hypothesis on $X$. ◻

</details>

Uniform sampling in this proof requires only the indicated marginal distributions; the maps $j_n$ need not be independent.

The maximal density lets us separate each initial span in a transfinite enumeration of $D$ from a point outside that span. The resulting dual functional annihilates every earlier point of the enumeration. Thus, at any fixed point, the exceptional indices form a set of cardinality less than $\kappa$ and have measure zero.

<a id="stmt-pos-family"></a>
<div id="pos:family" class="lemma">

<a id="pos:seminormalized"></a>
<a id="pos:pointwise-null"></a>
**Lemma 8**. *Let $X$ and $E$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#pos:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:density">7</a>. There are $\varepsilon>0$ and a family $(h_\alpha)_{\alpha<\kappa}$ in $S_{E^{**}}$ such that the functionals $f_\alpha=h_\alpha\circ R_E\in X^*$ satisfy $$\label{pos:seminormalized}
 \varepsilon<\|f_\alpha\|\le1\qquad(\alpha<\kappa),$$ and, for every $y\in R_E(B_X)$, $$\label{pos:pointwise-null}
 \mu\{\alpha:h_\alpha(y)\ne0\}=0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pos-family"><summary>Proof of stmt-pos-family</summary>

*Proof.* Write $D=R_E(B_X)$. There is a $\delta$-separated subset of $D$ of cardinality $\kappa$ for some $\delta>0$. Otherwise, choose a maximal $1/n$-separated subset for each $n\ge1$. Each would have cardinality less than $\kappa$, and their union would be dense in $D$ with cardinality less than $\kappa$, by regularity. This contradicts Lemma <a href="#pos:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:density">7</a>.

Enumerate $D=(y_\beta)_{\beta<\kappa}$. For $\alpha<\kappa$, let $M_\alpha=[y_\beta:\beta<\alpha]\subset E^*$. Its density is less than $\kappa$. Some $y\in D$ has distance greater than $\delta/4$ from $M_\alpha$: if every point of the separated set had distance at most $\delta/4$, a dense subset of $M_\alpha$ of cardinality less than $\kappa$ would provide centers at distance less than $3\delta/8$ from all those points. Two points cannot share such a center, a contradiction. Hahn–Banach gives $h_\alpha\in S_{E^{**}}$ annihilating $M_\alpha$ and satisfying $|h_\alpha(y)|>\delta/4$. Since $y=R_Ex$ for some $x\in B_X$, the functional $f_\alpha=h_\alpha R_E$ satisfies <a href="#pos:seminormalized" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:seminormalized">[pos:seminormalized]</a> with $\varepsilon=\delta/4$. For $y=y_\beta$, its nonzero evaluations occur only at indices $\alpha\le\beta$, a set of cardinality less than $\kappa$ and therefore $\mu$-null. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Borel coding and ordered sampling

We next explain how to use <a href="#pos:pointwise-null" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:pointwise-null">[pos:pointwise-null]</a> when the vector being evaluated is itself chosen using other indices. This is the only place where an interchange of integrals is needed. Borel evaluation also plays a central role in the perfect-unconditionality theorem of Argyros, Dodos and Kanellopoulos (Argyros, Dodos, and Kanellopoulos 2008, Theorem 4) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[ArgyrosDodosKanellopoulos2008](#ref-ArgyrosDodosKanellopoulos2008)]. Here it permits an ordered sampling argument using ordinary Fubini. The next lemma needs only a countably additive measure; the stronger additivity was used to produce the family to which it will be applied.

<a id="stmt-pos-sampling"></a>
<div id="pos:sampling" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *Let $X$ be a real Banach space, let $E\subset X^*$ be a separable closed subspace containing no isomorphic copy of $\ell_1$, and put $D=R_E(B_X)$. Let $I$ carry a countably additive probability $\mu$ on $\mathcal P(I)$ that vanishes on singletons. Suppose that $(h_\alpha)_{\alpha\in I}\subset E^{**}$ satisfies $\mu\{\alpha\in I:h_\alpha(y)\ne0\}=0$ for every $y\in D$, and that $f_\alpha=h_\alpha R_E$ satisfies $0<\varepsilon\le\|f_\alpha\|\le M<\infty$ for every $\alpha\in I$. Then $X^*$ contains an infinite unconditional basic sequence.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pos-sampling"><summary>Proof of stmt-pos-sampling</summary>

*Proof.* By the Odell–Rosenthal Theorem (Odell and Rosenthal 1975) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[OdellRosenthal1975](#ref-OdellRosenthal1975)], each $h_\alpha$ is the weak-star limit of a sequence from $E$. Choose one such sequence $c_\alpha\in E^{\mathbb N}$. Equip $E^{\mathbb N}$ with the product of the norm topologies and $K=B_{E^*}$ with the weak-star topology; both are Polish spaces, and $K$ is compact. There is a Borel function $$\Phi:E^{\mathbb N}\times K\longrightarrow\mathbb R$$ whose value at $(c,y)$ is $\lim_n y(c_n)$ if this limit exists and is zero otherwise. Indeed each map $(c,y)\mapsto y(c_n)$ is continuous, and the Cauchy criterion makes its convergence set Borel. To check the joint continuity, use $|y'(e')-y(e)|\le\|e'-e\|+|y'(e)-y(e)|$ for $y,y'\in K$.

The map $\alpha\mapsto c_\alpha$ is measurable because its domain has the full power-set sigma algebra. Let $\eta$ be its Borel pushforward probability on $E^{\mathbb N}$. The function <a id="pos:H"></a>$$\label{pos:H}
 H(y)=\int_{E^{\mathbb N}}\mathbf1_{\{\Phi(c,y)\ne0\}}\,d\eta(c)$$ is Borel on $K$, by parameter integration of a bounded Borel function. Our chosen codes give $\Phi(c_\alpha,y)=h_\alpha(y)$, so $H(y)=0$ for every $y\in D$.

For $n\ge1$ define a probability $\lambda_n$ on all subsets of $I^n$ by ordered iteration, with the first variable outermost: $$\lambda_n(A)=\int_I\cdots\int_I
      \mathbf1_A(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_n)
      \,d\mu(\alpha_n)\cdots d\mu(\alpha_1).$$ Put $\lambda_0$ equal to the unit mass on the one empty tuple. Every section and every intermediate integrand is measurable. Induction and monotone convergence prove countable additivity of $\lambda_n$; approximation by simple functions proves the same ordered integral formula for bounded functions. This construction does not assert that changing the order of these power-set integrals preserves their value.

<a id="pos:normer"></a>
<a id="pos:cross-vanishing"></a>
Fix $n$, rational coefficients $a_1,\ldots,a_n$, and a subset $s\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}$. For each tuple $(\alpha_j)_{j\in s}$ choose $x_s\in B_X$ such that $$\label{pos:normer}
 \left|\sum_{j\in s}a_j f_{\alpha_j}(x_s)\right|
 \ge\frac12\left\|\sum_{j\in s}a_j f_{\alpha_j}\right\|.$$ Choose $x_s=0$ if that sum is zero. The choice depends only on the indices in $s$. For $i\notin s$ we claim $$\label{pos:cross-vanishing}
 f_{\alpha_i}(x_s)=0\quad\text{for $\lambda_n$-almost every tuple.}$$

Fix any earlier prefix $(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_{i-1})$. The map $$Y:I^{n-i}\longrightarrow K,\qquad
 Y(\alpha_{i+1},\ldots,\alpha_n)=R_Ex_s$$ is independent of $\alpha_i$ and takes its values in $D$. It is measurable on the full power-set domain. Let $\nu=Y_*\lambda_{n-i}$, with the same interpretation when $i=n$. The ordered integral, starting at variable $i$, of the indicator of a nonzero evaluation in <a href="#pos:cross-vanishing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:cross-vanishing">[pos:cross-vanishing]</a> is $$\begin{aligned}
 &\int_I\int_{I^{n-i}}
    \mathbf1_{\{\Phi(c_{\alpha_i},Y(t))\ne0\}}
           \,d\lambda_{n-i}(t)\,d\mu(\alpha_i)\\
 &\qquad=\int_{E^{\mathbb N}}\int_K
    \mathbf1_{\{\Phi(c,y)\ne0\}}\,d\nu(y)\,d\eta(c)
   =\int_K H(y)\,d\nu(y)=0.
\end{aligned}$$ The change of order in the last line is ordinary Fubini for a Borel function on the product of two Polish spaces with Borel probability measures. The final equality does not require $D$ to be Borel: the Borel set $\{H\ne0\}$ has empty inverse image under $Y$, so is $\nu$-null. Since the earlier prefix was arbitrary, integrating over those variables proves <a href="#pos:cross-vanishing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:cross-vanishing">[pos:cross-vanishing]</a>.

<a id="pos:suppression"></a>
Combining <a href="#pos:normer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:normer">[pos:normer]</a> and <a href="#pos:cross-vanishing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:cross-vanishing">[pos:cross-vanishing]</a> gives $$\label{pos:suppression}
 \left\|\sum_{j\in s}a_j f_{\alpha_j}\right\|
 \le2\left\|\sum_{j=1}^n a_j f_{\alpha_j}\right\|$$ almost surely. Also the coordinates are pairwise distinct almost surely: for $r<t$, the event $\alpha_t=\alpha_r$ constrains variable $t$ to a singleton after its preceding variables have been fixed. There are countably many rational coefficient tuples and finitely many subsets $s$. Consequently, for each $n$ there is a set $G_n\subset I^n$ of $\lambda_n$-measure one on which distinctness and all these inequalities hold.

We must choose one sequence whose prefixes all lie in these sets. Suppose a prefix $p=(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_m)$ has been chosen with all its nonempty prefixes in their respective $G_j$, and with <a id="pos:prefix-invariant"></a>$$\label{pos:prefix-invariant}
 \lambda_{n-m}((G_n)_p)=1\qquad\text{for every }n>m.$$ Here $(G_n)_p$ denotes the section obtained by fixing the prefix $p$. The invariant holds for the empty prefix. For $n=m+1$, it says that $(p,\alpha)\in G_{m+1}$ for almost every $\alpha$. For every $n>m+1$, the definition of the ordered measures gives $$1=\lambda_{n-m}((G_n)_p)
  =\int_I\lambda_{n-m-1}((G_n)_{(p,\alpha)})\,d\mu(\alpha).$$ The integrand lies in $[0,1]$, so it equals $1$ for almost every $\alpha$. The intersection of these countably many conull sets is nonempty. Choose the next index there. This constructs the required infinite sequence without an infinite product measure.

Continuity extends <a href="#pos:suppression" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:suppression">[pos:suppression]</a> to real coefficients on every finite prefix. Singleton suppressions and $\|f_{\alpha_j}\|\ge\varepsilon$ give linear independence. The initial-segment projections have norm at most $2$ on the algebraic span and extend to its closure. Approximation by finite sums shows that these projections converge to the identity, so the selected sequence is a Schauder basis for its closed span. Every finite coordinate suppression also has norm at most $2$. For a vector approximated within $\delta$ by a finite sum, every finite coordinate sum outside that sum’s support has norm at most $2\delta$. It follows that the basis expansion converges unconditionally. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### The positive conclusion

<a id="stmt-pos-theorem"></a>
<div id="pos:theorem" class="theorem">

**Theorem 10**. *Suppose that $\mathfrak c$ carries a $\mathfrak c$-additive probability measure on its full power set that vanishes on singletons, with additivity understood for disjoint families of cardinality strictly less than $\mathfrak c$. Then every infinite-dimensional real Banach space has a separable infinite-dimensional quotient.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pos-theorem"><summary>Proof of stmt-pos-theorem</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $X$ is a counterexample. A consequence of the Hagler–Johnson Theorem, in the precise form of (Argyros, Dodos, and Kanellopoulos 2008, Proposition 16) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ArgyrosDodosKanellopoulos2008](#ref-ArgyrosDodosKanellopoulos2008)], says that an unconditional basic sequence in $X^*$ gives a separable infinite-dimensional quotient of $X$. Thus $X^*$ contains no such sequence. Choose an infinite-dimensional separable closed subspace $E\subset X^*$. It contains no isomorphic copy of $\ell_1$, whose usual basis would give an unconditional basic sequence in $X^*$.

Lemmas <a href="#pos:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:density">7</a> and <a href="#pos:family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:family">8</a> give a seminormalized family $f_\alpha=h_\alpha R_E$ with the pointwise null-support property. Lemma <a href="#pos:sampling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:sampling">9</a> then produces an unconditional basic sequence in $X^*$, the desired contradiction. ◻

</details>

The real positive implication is now complete. The next section transfers it to complex Banach spaces; Section <a href="#model:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:section">10</a> supplies a model of its measure hypothesis from a measurable cardinal.

<a id="sec:fields"></a>
### Passing between real and complex spaces

Theorem <a href="#pos:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:theorem">10</a> has established the positive implication for real Banach spaces. We now transfer it to complex spaces. We also prepare the transfer needed for the negative implication: after constructing a real counterexample, we will take its complexification. The two transfers require different arguments.

<a id="stmt-ev-positive-transfer"></a>
<div id="ev:positive-transfer" class="proposition">

**Proposition 11**. *Let $X$ be an infinite-dimensional complex Banach space, and let $X_{\mathbb R}$ be its underlying real space. If $X_{\mathbb R}$ has a separable infinite-dimensional real quotient, then $X$ has a separable infinite-dimensional complex quotient.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ev-positive-transfer"><summary>Proof of stmt-ev-positive-transfer</summary>

*Proof.* The map $$A:(X_{\mathbb R})^*\longrightarrow (X^*)_{\mathbb R},\qquad
 (Ag)(x)=g(x)-i g(ix),$$ is a real-linear isometry with inverse $f\mapsto\operatorname{Re}f$. Indeed $Ag$ is complex-linear, and phase rotation in $B_X$ proves $\|Ag\|=\|g\|$.
